I mean, I think about work and things I need to get done, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around anxious or worried all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with your memory?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed memory trouble, no. It’s more like I get stuck deciding or focusing, but I’m not forgetting people’s names or where I put things any more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have problems with focus or decision-making been present since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not since I was a child. This feels more like something going on now. I’ll sit with a board packet or a vendor email and my head just feels cloudy, like I can’t make even a simple decision that I used to handle pretty quickly.\n\nDoctor: Did this cloudy thinking start after a medical illness or injury?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember having any illness or injury right before it started. It just kind of came on, from what I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a major stressful event or change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it did. It’s been about two weeks, since my husband retired and started being home all day. Around then is when I noticed this sharper change in my energy and confidence, and then that low, cloudy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I haven’t really wanted to go to my dance class, or look around at the little galleries like I usually do. Even cooking Sunday food for my family… I just don’t feel like doing it, and those are things that normally make me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say sad most days. I’ve felt kind of low and off, but I’m not sure it’s sadness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a big change with my sleep, at least nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems been interfering with your ability to do your job?\n\nPatient: Yes, a little. I’m still going to work, but I’ve had to slow myself down on decisions. I haven’t really wanted to go to my dance class, or look around at the little galleries like I usually do. Even cooking Sunday food for my family… I just don’t feel like doing it, and those are things that normally make me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say sad most days. I haven’t been eating much during the day. I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and that kind of carries me until later. It’s more like I get stuck deciding or focusing, but I’m not forgetting people’s names or where I put things any more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have problems with focus or decision-making been present since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not since I was a child. This feels more like something going on now. I’ll sit with a board packet or a vendor email and my head just feels cloudy, like I can’t make even a simple decision that I used to handle pretty quickly.\n\nDoctor: Did this cloudy thinking start after a medical illness or injury?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember having any illness or injury right before it started. It just kind of came on, from what I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a major stressful event or change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it did. It’s been about two weeks, since my husband retired and started being home all day. Around then is when I noticed this sharper change in my energy and confidence, and then that low, cloudy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes. I haven’t really wanted to go to my dance class, or look around at the little galleries like I usually do. Even cooking Sunday food for my family… I just don’t feel like doing it, and those are things that normally make me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say sad most days. I’ve felt kind of low and off, but I’m not sure it’s sadness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve been asking my assistant to double-check vendor details, and I put off a couple of board items because I don’t really trust my judgment right now.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve felt kind of low at times, but I don’t think I’d say down or depressed most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Does this change with your husband being home all day feel very stressful or hard to cope with for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it very stressful. It’s an adjustment having him around all day, but I can’t say it’s been something I can’t cope with.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a reduced appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I haven’t been eating much during the day. I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and that kind of carries me until later. Then at night I might pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say a lot of distress. It bothers me some, sure, but it’s not like I’m falling apart over it. But then in meetings it’s almost the opposite—I catch myself tapping my pen or shifting around in the chair, like I’m too keyed up to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: A few mornings, yes… I’ve had the thought that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. It scares me to say that. But I don’t want to hurt myself, and I don’t have any plan to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you gotten lost or confused about where you were, what day it was, or what time it was?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t gotten lost or not known where I was. I haven’t noticed a big change with my sleep, at least nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems been interfering with your ability to do your job?\n\nPatient: Yes, a little. I’m still going to work, but I’ve had to slow myself down on decisions. I’ve been asking my assistant to double-check vendor details, and I put off a couple of board items because I don’t really trust my judgment right now.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve felt kind of low at times, but I don’t think I’d say down or depressed most of the day nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Does this change with your husband being home all day feel very stressful or hard to cope with for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it very stressful. It’s an adjustment having him around all day, but I can’t say it’s been something I can’t cope with.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a reduced appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I haven’t been eating much during the day. I’ll have coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and that kind of carries me until later. Then at night I might pick at tortillas or something sweet, but I’m not really enjoying it.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say a lot of distress. It bothers me some, sure, but it’s not like I’m falling apart over it. Just kind of… not feeling quite right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: At home, yes, I feel like I move slower, like my legs are heavy. But then in meetings it’s almost the opposite—I catch myself tapping my pen or shifting around in the chair, like I’m too keyed up to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: A few mornings, yes… I’ve had the thought that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. It scares me to say that. But I don’t want to hurt myself, and I don’t have any plan to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you gotten lost or confused about where you were, what day it was, or what time it was?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t gotten lost or not known where I was. I might forget what day it is for a second if I’m tired, but nothing like being confused about it.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present nearly every day for the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much. This sharper change in my energy and confidence, and then the low, cloudy feeling after, has been going on about two weeks now—since my husband retired and was suddenly home all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve felt worn down some days, but I haven’t been paying close attention to whether it’s unusual or all the time."
